========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I came in because I just feel worn out all the time. It’s like, even after a full night in bed, my body feels like it’s made of sandbags. Just getting through a workday or, you know, folding a basket of laundry takes absolutely everything I have. 

Speaking of laundry, I actually had to switch detergents recently because the one I usually buy was completely out of stock at the grocery store. I ended up getting this lavender-scented one, which smells okay, but it strongly reminds me of my aunt's house in Ohio. We used to drive out there every summer when I was a kid, and she had these huge lavender bushes right by the front porch. The bees were always everywhere, which absolutely terrified my younger sister. She would just scream and refuse to get out of the station wagon until my dad swatted them away with a newspaper... 

But anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just don't have the energy for anything lately.

I know you're trying to be really thorough, and I promise I'm thinking hard about it, but no. Truly, no. 

Even when I've taken a few days off just to do absolutely nothing, I usually can't even sit still. Like, my friend Sarah convinced me to go to this three-day silent meditation retreat upstate a couple of years ago. She swore it would completely reset my stress levels. But let me tell you, being completely silent for three days is actually torture. You're just sitting there on these stiff little round cushions—zafus, I think they call them—and my lower back was absolutely screaming by the first afternoon. Plus, they only fed us this strict macrobiotic vegan stuff. I have nothing against vegetables, but when you're eating unseasoned steamed kale and plain brown rice for breakfast, it really tests your sanity. Sarah kept making these serene, enlightened faces at me across the dining hall, and I just wanted a cup of actual, caffeinated coffee so badly. I actually ended up sneaking out to my car on the second day just to listen to a podcast and eat a stale granola bar I found in my glove compartment... 

Oh, gosh, sorry. I'm doing it again, aren't I? Getting totally off track. But yeah, to answer you—I wasn't depressed at that retreat, just incredibly bored and hungry. Before these last two months, I really haven't ever had a period where I felt that dark, heavy exhaustion. Not even for a weekend.

Um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, I've been super stressed before, obviously. Like a couple of years ago when we did this massive Q3 report for a major beverage company. Which is so frustrating, because honestly, the marketing trends for Gen Z are just baffling to me sometimes. My niece told me the laughing-crying emoji is basically social suicide now, which makes absolutely no sense because how else are you supposed to show something is funny? A skull? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I really, really haven't. I swear I'm not hiding anything from you. I just haven't ever felt like this before. 

Even when things were bad, I always had energy. Like when my dog, Buster, passed away. He was a golden retriever mix, the absolute sweetest boy. We rescued him when he was already five, and he had this funny little limp from an old injury, but it never stopped him from trying to chase the squirrels in the backyard. The squirrels around my neighborhood are incredibly bold, by the way. One of them actually chewed through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. I had to hire a guy to come fix the screen, and he charged me an arm and a leg. He said it was because it was some special heavy-duty pet screen, but honestly, I think he just saw a woman living alone and decided to upcharge me. It's so hard to find reliable contractors these days. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, tried to get her roof redone and the crew just stopped showing up halfway through the job. She had a blue tarp over her house for like three months...

Wait, sorry. I'm doing it again. But no, even when Buster died and I was so sad, I didn't lose my energy. I was crying, sure, but I was still getting up, going to work, walking his normal route just out of habit. I never had this... this total drain where I just can't care about anything. Not even for a day.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah... I have. A lot, actually. I just keep replaying this mortgage decision we made, and I keep telling myself I was so selfish and reckless. Like I put our whole household at risk. 

Which is stressful enough without dealing with the bank. Have you tried calling a bank lately? You can never get a real human being on the phone anymore. It's just that automated voice telling you to press one for checking, press two for loans. And then when you finally get through, the connection is always terrible. The guy I spoke to last week sounded like he was in a wind tunnel. I actually used to work on a marketing campaign for a telecommunications company a few years ago, and we did this whole focus group on hold music. Turns out people absolutely hate smooth jazz, they'd rather just hear silence or classical. We ended up recommending this upbeat acoustic guitar track based on all the data, but the client went with the jazz anyway. Typical, right? You pay for all this market research and then just completely ignore the analysts... 

Oh, gosh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But yeah... the guilt is just eating at me. Even though my husband constantly reminds me that we made the mortgage decision together, I just can't stop feeling like it's all my fault and I've ruined everything for us.

No, this is... this is really new. I've never felt this kind of crushing guilt before. Like I said, I just keep replaying that mortgage decision over and over in my head. I keep telling myself I was so selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk. 

And you know, we bought this house because it has this incredible backyard with these old oak trees. They're gorgeous, especially in the fall when the leaves change. But nobody tells you how much maintenance old trees take. We had to have an arborist come out last year because one of the branches was hanging right over the power lines. The guy showed up in this massive bucket truck, and he was telling me all about how oak wilt disease is spreading through the neighborhood. Apparently, you're not supposed to prune them between February and June because these little beetles carry the fungus. It's fascinating, really, how an entire ecosystem can be threatened by one tiny insect. I ended up reading this whole article about it in National Geographic. I've had a subscription since I was a kid—my grandfather used to keep stacks of them in his basement. They smelled like dust and old paper, you know that smell? 

Sorry, I'm doing it again. I'm sorry. But yeah, to answer your question, this guilt is new. Even though my husband keeps reminding me that we made the mortgage decision together, I just can't shake this feeling that I'm the one who ruined everything. I've never felt this worthless before.

I had to dig through my filing cabinet to find the original survey paperwork just to get him to leave me alone. He's always finding something to nitpick... \n\nBut, um, anyway. What was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah. I just sit at that table and look out the window and... I just feel like there is no good version of the future coming. Just that heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt this way—like you couldn’t enjoy anything and had very little energy—for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... this whole depressed, slowed-down, exhausted part has been going on most days for about the past two months. I know it started right around the time I was running this big focus group for work. \n\nI'm a market research analyst, and we had this new client—a premium pet food brand. They wanted us to test out the messaging for these new artisanal dog treats. I mean, they had ingredients like organic quinoa and sustainably caught whitefish. For dogs! It's wild how much people will spend on their pets now. My sister's golden retriever eats literally anything, including my favorite pair of leather sandals last Thanksgiving. She didn't even offer to replace them, which really stung because I bought them on a trip to Mexico and you just can't find that kind of tooling here. The leather was so incredibly soft, and they took weeks to break in perfectly. I tried looking online for the same artisan, but the website was all in Spanish and the shipping was going to be astronomical... \n\nBut, um, anyway. What were we talking about? Oh, right, the two weeks thing. Yeah, it's been way longer than two weeks. Like I said, it's been a solid two months of just feeling totally drained and down.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt this way—down, exhausted, and unable to enjoy things—for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before this current one? Um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, I've been super stressed before, obviously. Like a couple of years ago when we did this massive Q3 report for a major beverage company. They completely changed their demographic targets at the literal last minute. We had to throw out weeks of data because suddenly they only cared about Gen Z consumers instead of Millennials. Which is so frustrating, because honestly, the marketing trends for Gen Z are just baffling to me sometimes. Everything has to be a short video with some trending audio, and if you use the wrong emoji, you're suddenly completely out of touch. My niece told me the laughing-crying emoji is basically social suicide now, which makes absolutely no sense because how else are you supposed to show something is funny? A skull? It's just morbid, honestly. I don't understand why we have to constantly reinvent perfectly good ways to communicate just to feel young... \n\nBut, oh, sorry. You were asking if I'd felt this down before? No, even with all that work stress, I never felt this heavy or exhausted. This is really the first time it's been like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other episodes in your life, before this current one, where you felt persistently down, lost interest in things, and had very little energy—even if it was for a shorter time?\n\nPatient: No, not even for a shorter time. I mean, sure, I've had a day or two where I was just totally drained, like when I caught that awful stomach bug after a marketing conference in Vegas a few years ago. Have you ever been to one of those massive trade shows at the convention center? It is just miles and miles of walking on that terrible, thin patterned carpet. My feet were throbbing so badly by day three. And the air conditioning is always blasting, so you're freezing inside but it's like a hundred degrees outside on the strip. I swear that's how I got sick, the temperature fluctuations just completely wreck your immune system. I tried telling my boss we should just do virtual booths for the smaller expos, but he insists on the whole face-to-face networking thing. Which usually just means people grabbing our free branded tote bags and walking away without even looking at our brochures. We ordered these really nice canvas bags last year, too, not the cheap flimsy ones that rip if you put a laptop in them. I actually kept one for my groceries, but the cashier at the store spilled a whole container of blueberries in it and it completely stained the bottom... \n\nUm, anyway. Sorry. No, to answer your question, I haven't ever felt this kind of persistent, heavy depression before, not even for a few days. It's really just been this current stretch.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had any other periods in your life where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had very little energy, even if it was just for a few days?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to be really thorough, and I promise I'm thinking hard about it, but no. Truly, no. \n\nEven when I've taken a few days off just to do absolutely nothing, I usually can't even sit still. Like, my friend Sarah convinced me to go to this three-day silent meditation retreat upstate a couple of years ago. She swore it would completely reset my stress levels. But let me tell you, being completely silent for three days is actually torture. You're just sitting there on these stiff little round cushions—zafus, I think they call them—and my lower back was absolutely screaming by the first afternoon. Plus, they only fed us this strict macrobiotic vegan stuff. I have nothing against vegetables, but when you're eating unseasoned steamed kale and plain brown rice for breakfast, it really tests your sanity. Sarah kept making these serene, enlightened faces at me across the dining hall, and I just wanted a cup of actual, caffeinated coffee so badly. I actually ended up sneaking out to my car on the second day just to listen to a podcast and eat a stale granola bar I found in my glove compartment... \n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I'm doing it again, aren't I? Getting totally off track. But yeah, to answer you—I wasn't depressed at that retreat, just incredibly bored and hungry. Before these last two months, I really haven't ever had a period where I felt that dark, heavy exhaustion. Not even for a weekend.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had any brief periods—maybe just a few days or even less—where you felt down, lost interest in things, or had very little energy?\n\nPatient: I really, really haven't. I swear I'm not hiding anything from you. I just haven't ever felt like this before. \n\nEven when things were bad, I always had energy. Like when my dog, Buster, passed away. He was a golden retriever mix, the absolute sweetest boy. We rescued him when he was already five, and he had this funny little limp from an old injury, but it never stopped him from trying to chase the squirrels in the backyard. The squirrels around my neighborhood are incredibly bold, by the way. One of them actually chewed through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. I had to hire a guy to come fix the screen, and he charged me an arm and a leg. He said it was because it was some special heavy-duty pet screen, but honestly, I think he just saw a woman living alone and decided to upcharge me. He said it was because it was some special heavy-duty pet screen, but honestly, I think he just saw a woman living alone and decided to upcharge me. It's so hard to find reliable contractors these days. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, tried to get her roof redone and the crew just stopped showing up halfway through the job. She had a blue tarp over her house for like three months...\n\nWait, sorry. I'm doing it again. But no, even when Buster died and I was so sad, I didn't lose my energy. I was crying, sure, but I was still getting up, going to work, walking his normal route just out of habit. I never had this... this total drain where I just can't care about anything. Not even for a day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately, like you're a burden or that you've let people down?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I have. A lot, actually. I just keep replaying this mortgage decision we made, and I keep telling myself I was so selfish and reckless. Like I put our whole household at risk. \n\nWhich is stressful enough without dealing with the bank. Have you tried calling a bank lately? You can never get a real human being on the phone anymore. It's just that automated voice telling you to press one for checking, press two for loans. And then when you finally get through, the connection is always terrible. The guy I spoke to last week sounded like he was in a wind tunnel. I actually used to work on a marketing campaign for a telecommunications company a few years ago, and we did this whole focus group on hold music. Turns out people absolutely hate smooth jazz, they'd rather just hear silence or classical. We ended up recommending this upbeat acoustic guitar track based on all the data, but the client went with the jazz anyway. Typical, right? You pay for all this market research and then just completely ignore the analysts... \n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But yeah... the guilt is just eating at me. Even though my husband constantly reminds me that we made the mortgage decision together, I just can't stop feeling like it's all my fault and I've ruined everything for us.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced feelings of worthlessness or guilt like this before, or is this something new with the current episode?\n\nPatient: No, this is... this is really new. I've never felt this kind of crushing guilt before. Like I said, I just keep replaying that mortgage decision over and over in my head. I keep telling myself I was so selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk. \n\nAnd you know, we bought this house because it has this incredible backyard with these old oak trees. They're gorgeous, especially in the fall when the leaves change. But nobody tells you how much maintenance old trees take. We had to have an arborist come out last year because one of the branches was hanging right over the power lines. The guy showed up in this massive bucket truck, and he was telling me all about how oak wilt disease is spreading through the neighborhood. Apparently, you're not supposed to prune them between February and June because these little beetles carry the fungus. It's fascinating, really, how an entire ecosystem can be threatened by one tiny insect. I ended up reading this whole article about it in National Geographic. I've had a subscription since I was a kid—my grandfather used to keep stacks of them in his basement. They smelled like dust and old paper, you know that smell? \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again. Even though my husband keeps reminding me that we made the mortgage decision together, I just can't shake this feeling that I'm the one who ruined everything. I know it started right around the time I was running this big focus group for work. \n\nI'm a market research analyst, and we had this new client—a premium pet food brand. They wanted us to test out the messaging for these new artisanal dog treats. I mean, they had ingredients like organic quinoa and sustainably caught whitefish. For dogs! It's wild how much people will spend on their pets now. My sister's golden retriever eats literally anything, including my favorite pair of leather sandals last Thanksgiving. She didn't even offer to replace them, which really stung because I bought them on a trip to Mexico and you just can't find that kind of tooling here. The leather was so incredibly soft, and they took weeks to break in perfectly. I tried looking online for the same artisan, but the website was all in Spanish and the shipping was going to be astronomical... \n\nBut, um, anyway. What were we talking about? Oh, right, the two weeks thing. Yeah, it's been way longer than two weeks. Like I said, it's been a solid two months of just feeling totally drained and down.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt this way—down, exhausted, and unable to enjoy things—for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before this current one? Um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, I've been super stressed before, obviously. Like a couple of years ago when we did this massive Q3 report for a major beverage company. They completely changed their demographic targets at the literal last minute. We had to throw out weeks of data because suddenly they only cared about Gen Z consumers instead of Millennials. Which is so frustrating, because honestly, the marketing trends for Gen Z are just baffling to me sometimes. Everything has to be a short video with some trending audio, and if you use the wrong emoji, you're suddenly completely out of touch. My niece told me the laughing-crying emoji is basically social suicide now, which makes absolutely no sense because how else are you supposed to show something is funny? A skull? It's just morbid, honestly. I don't understand why we have to constantly reinvent perfectly good ways to communicate just to feel young... \n\nBut, oh, sorry. You were asking if I'd felt this down before? No, even with all that work stress, I never felt this heavy or exhausted. This is really the first time it's been like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other episodes in your life, before this current one, where you felt persistently down, lost interest in things, and had very little energy—even if it was for a shorter time?\n\nPatient: No, not even for a shorter time. My feet were throbbing so badly by day three. And the air conditioning is always blasting, so you're freezing inside but it's like a hundred degrees outside on the strip. I swear that's how I got sick, the temperature fluctuations just completely wreck your immune system. I tried telling my boss we should just do virtual booths for the smaller expos, but he insists on the whole face-to-face networking thing. Which usually just means people grabbing our free branded tote bags and walking away without even looking at our brochures. We ordered these really nice canvas bags last year, too, not the cheap flimsy ones that rip if you put a laptop in them. I actually kept one for my groceries, but the cashier at the store spilled a whole container of blueberries in it and it completely stained the bottom... \n\nUm, anyway. Sorry. No, never. I mean, two weeks is a long time to feel like that. Actually, two weeks is exactly how long we usually run our A/B tests at work to get statistical significance. I was managing this email campaign for a local bakery a while back, and we ran a two-week test on their newsletter. We were trying to figure out if people clicked more on a green "Order Now" button or a red one. You'd think red, right? Because it creates urgency. But actually, the green one got way more clicks. I think it's because green feels like "go" or maybe it's just less aggressive on the eyes in the morning. Color psychology is so wild. I took a whole class on it when I was getting my associate's degree. Did you know fast food places almost always use yellow and red because it supposedly makes you hungry and subconsciously encourages you to eat quickly and leave? It's kind of manipulative when you really think about it...

No, I really haven't. I know you've asked me this a few different ways now, but the answer is still no. I never had any kind of episode like that, not for two weeks, not for less than two weeks, not even for a day. 

It's actually kind of frustrating, trying to figure out why this is happening now, because I've always been the person who just keeps going. Like the Energizer Bunny. Do they even still make those commercials? With the pink bunny and the drum? I always wondered who came up with that mascot. Like, why a bunny? I ended up getting this lavender-scented one, which smells okay, but it strongly reminds me of my aunt's house in Ohio. We used to drive out there every summer when I was a kid, and she had these huge lavender bushes right by the front porch. The bees were always everywhere, which absolutely terrified my younger sister. She would just scream and refuse to get out of the station wagon until my dad swatted them away with a newspaper... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just don't have the energy for anything lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been feeling down or losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. It's like... most days I just wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling right in my chest. I'll get up and just sit at the kitchen table, staring out at the yard for I don't even know how long. \n\nWhich reminds me, I really need to get a tree service out there. We had that weird windstorm last week and a massive branch came down from the old oak tree. It barely missed the neighbor's fence, which is a huge relief because Dave—he's the guy next door—is so incredibly particular about his property line. He actually came over last spring with a literal tape measure when I was trying to put in some raised garden beds. A tape measure! I had to dig through my filing cabinet to find the original survey paperwork just to get him to leave me alone. He's always finding something to nitpick... \n\nBut, um, anyway. What was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah. I just sit at that table and look out the window and... I just feel like there is no good version of the future coming. Just that heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt this way—like you couldn’t enjoy anything and had very little energy—for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... this whole depressed, slowed-down, exhausted part has been going on most days for about the past two months. I know it started right around the time I was running this big focus group for work. \n\nI'm a market research analyst, and we had this new client—a premium pet food brand. They wanted us to test out the messaging for these new artisanal dog treats. I mean, they had ingredients like organic quinoa and sustainably caught whitefish. For dogs! It's wild how much people will spend on their pets now. My sister's golden retriever eats literally anything, including my favorite pair of leather sandals last Thanksgiving. She didn't even offer to replace them, which really stung because I bought them on a trip to Mexico and you just can't find that kind of tooling here. The leather was so incredibly soft, and they took weeks to break in perfectly. I tried looking online for the same artisan, but the website was all in Spanish and the shipping was going to be astronomical... \n\nBut, um, anyway. What were we talking about? Oh, right, the two weeks thing. Yeah, it's been way longer than two weeks. Like I said, it's been a solid two months of just feeling totally drained and down.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt this way—down, exhausted, and unable to enjoy things—for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before this current one? Um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, I've been super stressed before, obviously. Like a couple of years ago when we did this massive Q3 report for a major beverage company. They completely changed their demographic targets at the literal last minute. We had to throw out weeks of data because suddenly they only cared about Gen Z consumers instead of Millennials. Which is so frustrating, because honestly, the marketing trends for Gen Z are just baffling to me sometimes. Everything has to be a short video with some trending audio, and if you use the wrong emoji, you're suddenly completely out of touch. My niece told me the laughing-crying emoji is basically social suicide now, which makes absolutely no sense because how else are you supposed to show something is funny? A skull? It's just morbid, honestly. I don't understand why we have to constantly reinvent perfectly good ways to communicate just to feel young... \n\nBut, oh, sorry. You were asking if I'd felt this down before? No, even with all that work stress, I never felt this heavy or exhausted. This is really the first time it's been like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other episodes in your life, before this current one, where you felt persistently down, lost interest in things, and had very little energy—even if it was for a shorter time?\n\nPatient: No, not even for a shorter time. I mean, sure, I've had a day or two where I was just totally drained, like when I caught that awful stomach bug after a marketing conference in Vegas a few years ago. Have you ever been to one of those massive trade shows at the convention center? It is just miles and miles of walking on that terrible, thin patterned carpet. My feet were throbbing so badly by day three. And the air conditioning is always blasting, so you're freezing inside but it's like a hundred degrees outside on the strip. I swear that's how I got sick, the temperature fluctuations just completely wreck your immune system. I tried telling my boss we should just do virtual booths for the smaller expos, but he insists on the whole face-to-face networking thing. Which usually just means people grabbing our free branded tote bags and walking away without even looking at our brochures. We ordered these really nice canvas bags last year, too, not the cheap flimsy ones that rip if you put a laptop in them. I actually kept one for my groceries, but the cashier at the store spilled a whole container of blueberries in it and it completely stained the bottom... \n\nUm, anyway. Sorry. No, to answer your question, I haven't ever felt this kind of persistent, heavy depression before, not even for a few days. It's really just been this current stretch.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had any other periods in your life where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had very little energy, even if it was just for a few days?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to be really thorough, and I promise I'm thinking hard about it, but no. Truly, no. \n\nEven when I've taken a few days off just to do absolutely nothing, I usually can't even sit still. Like, my friend Sarah convinced me to go to this three-day silent meditation retreat upstate a couple of years ago. She swore it would completely reset my stress levels. But let me tell you, being completely silent for three days is actually torture. You're just sitting there on these stiff little round cushions—zafus, I think they call them—and my lower back was absolutely screaming by the first afternoon. Plus, they only fed us this strict macrobiotic vegan stuff. I have nothing against vegetables, but when you're eating unseasoned steamed kale and plain brown rice for breakfast, it really tests your sanity. Sarah kept making these serene, enlightened faces at me across the dining hall, and I just wanted a cup of actual, caffeinated coffee so badly. I actually ended up sneaking out to my car on the second day just to listen to a podcast and eat a stale granola bar I found in my glove compartment... \n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I'm doing it again, aren't I? Getting totally off track. But yeah, to answer you—I wasn't depressed at that retreat, just incredibly bored and hungry. Before these last two months, I really haven't ever had a period where I felt that dark, heavy exhaustion. Not even for a weekend.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had any brief periods—maybe just a few days or even less—where you felt down, lost interest in things, or had very little energy?\n\nPatient: I really, really haven't. I swear I'm not hiding anything from you. I just haven't ever felt like this before. \n\nEven when things were bad, I always had energy. Like when my dog, Buster, passed away. He was a golden retriever mix, the absolute sweetest boy. We rescued him when he was already five, and he had this funny little limp from an old injury, but it never stopped him from trying to chase the squirrels in the backyard. The squirrels around my neighborhood are incredibly bold, by the way. One of them actually chewed through the screen on my porch last summer just to get to a bag of birdseed I'd left out. I had to hire a guy to come fix the screen, and he charged me an arm and a leg. He said it was because it was some special heavy-duty pet screen, but honestly, I think he just saw a woman living alone and decided to upcharge me. It's so hard to find reliable contractors these days. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, tried to get her roof redone and the crew just stopped showing up halfway through the job. She had a blue tarp over her house for like three months...\n\nWait, sorry. I'm doing it again. But no, even when Buster died and I was so sad, I didn't lose my energy. I was crying, sure, but I was still getting up, going to work, walking his normal route just out of habit. I never had this... this total drain where I just can't care about anything. Not even for a day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately, like you're a burden or that you've let people down?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I have. A lot, actually. I just keep replaying this mortgage decision we made, and I keep telling myself I was so selfish and reckless. Like I put our whole household at risk. \n\nWhich is stressful enough without dealing with the bank. Have you tried calling a bank lately? You can never get a real human being on the phone anymore. It's just that automated voice telling you to press one for checking, press two for loans. And then when you finally get through, the connection is always terrible. The guy I spoke to last week sounded like he was in a wind tunnel. I actually used to work on a marketing campaign for a telecommunications company a few years ago, and we did this whole focus group on hold music. Turns out people absolutely hate smooth jazz, they'd rather just hear silence or classical. We ended up recommending this upbeat acoustic guitar track based on all the data, but the client went with the jazz anyway. Typical, right? You pay for all this market research and then just completely ignore the analysts... \n\nOh, gosh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But yeah... the guilt is just eating at me. Even though my husband constantly reminds me that we made the mortgage decision together, I just can't stop feeling like it's all my fault and I've ruined everything for us.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced feelings of worthlessness or guilt like this before, or is this something new with the current episode?\n\nPatient: No, this is... this is really new. I've never felt this kind of crushing guilt before. Like I said, I just keep replaying that mortgage decision over and over in my head. I keep telling myself I was so selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk. \n\nAnd you know, we bought this house because it has this incredible backyard with these old oak trees. They're gorgeous, especially in the fall when the leaves change. But nobody tells you how much maintenance old trees take. We had to have an arborist come out last year because one of the branches was hanging right over the power lines. The guy showed up in this massive bucket truck, and he was telling me all about how oak wilt disease is spreading through the neighborhood. Apparently, you're not supposed to prune them between February and June because these little beetles carry the fungus. It's fascinating, really, how an entire ecosystem can be threatened by one tiny insect. I ended up reading this whole article about it in National Geographic. I've had a subscription since I was a kid—my grandfather used to keep stacks of them in his basement. They smelled like dust and old paper, you know that smell? \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again. I'm sorry. But yeah, to answer your question, this guilt is new. Even though my husband keeps reminding me that we made the mortgage decision together, I just can't shake this feeling that I'm the one who ruined everything. I tried planting tomatoes last spring—heirloom ones, the Cherokee Purples, because they just taste so much better than the ones you buy at the grocery store. No, I've never had any periods where I felt down or lost interest in things before this. I mean, two weeks is a long time to feel like that. Actually, two weeks is exactly how long we usually run our A/B tests at work to get statistical significance. I was managing this email campaign for a local bakery a while back, and we ran a two-week test on their newsletter. We were trying to figure out if people clicked more on a green \"Order Now\" button or a red one. You'd think red, right? Because it creates urgency. But actually, the green one got way more clicks. I think it's because green feels like \"go\" or maybe it's just less aggressive on the eyes in the morning. Color psychology is so wild. I took a whole class on it when I was getting my associate's degree. Did you know fast food places almost always use yellow and red because it supposedly makes you hungry and subconsciously encourages you to eat quickly and leave? Not like... emotional gravity, but actual gravity. My nephew was trying to explain black holes to me over Thanksgiving. He's twelve and obsessed with space. He was saying that the gravity is so intense that not even light can escape, which is just terrifying if you think about it too long. We were watching this documentary he put on, and they showed these artist renderings of what it looks like when a star gets pulled in. It stretches out like spaghetti. They actually call it spaghettification, which sounds like a fake word, doesn't it? Like something a toddler would make up. Like the Energizer Bunny. Do they even still make those commercials? With the pink bunny and the drum? I always wondered who came up with that mascot. Like, why a bunny? Why not a cheetah or something that's actually fast and has a lot of endurance? Though I guess bunnies do have a lot of energy. We had a pet rabbit when I was a kid, named Snowball—so original, right?—and he used to thump his back foot on the floor when he was annoyed. It was surprisingly loud for such a small animal. We had hardwood floors, which probably amplified the sound. My mom hated those floors because they showed every speck of dust. She was always sweeping.
